Guide Tracker All banks Six examples Meeting report

Six real API microbenchmark examples

These are six saved, executed R0 examples, one per library. Each includes the exact model request, response, wrapped executable program, console output, execution command, and recorded verdict. No new model calls or test executions were made to build this guide.

Scope. These examples belong to the current explicit-inputs-r0 protocol and use gemini-3.1-flash-lite. The audience writes both the API call and its assertions. A native pass means the generated program passed execution checks and emitted the required exact-target witness; it is not independent validation of all API behavior. These six examples were selected for readability and are not a representative performance sample. R1/R2 repairs, D2 revision, aliases, embedded hints, and puzzle evaluation are not part of these runs.

The RDPro walkthrough below uses Scala only. The separate Java bank and its earlier failure evidence remain available in the bank index and existing report evidence.

Open one example from beginning to end

  1. Open request.json to read the complete system and user messages, API target, selected documentation, supplied-variable descriptions, model, and output allowance.
  2. Open response.txt to see the exact returned program fragment. Then open the wrapped program to see its imports, initialized inputs, execution wrapper, and inserted fragment.
  3. Open INPUTS.json for fixture paths, hashes, preamble, and shared input contract. For mir_eval, reuse the full wrapped program and existing source evidence; the four intervals are shown below.
  4. Open execution.json, stdout.txt, and stderr.txt to see the command, elapsed execution time, exit status, output, and any diagnostics. provider.json separately records request time and tokens.
  5. Open VERDICT_AND_PROVENANCE.json (or the existing mir_eval evidence.json) for the native verdict, original result location, and file hashes.

RDPro (Scala)

Selected API: RasterMetadata. Documentation condition: Generated README D1.

Actual input. A synthetic raster extent with width 737 and height 628, 256×256 tiles, SRID 4326, and an explicitly constructed affine transform. The test creates metadata; it does not resize pixels or establish that its transform describes the supplied GeoTIFF.

What the saved program checks. The generated program checks the constructor fields against the values it supplied, then prints the extent pixel count.

Full prompt | LLM response | Complete executable program | Actual output | Errors/warnings

Execution command and time | Model time and tokens | Verdict and provenance

Fixture paths, hashes, and initialization

Exact returned fragment

import edu.ucr.cs.bdlab.beast.geolite.RasterMetadata
import java.awt.geom.AffineTransform

// Define parameters for a 737x628 raster (matching the provided rasterRDD dimensions)
val x1 = 0
val y1 = 0
val x2 = 737
val y2 = 628
val tileWidth = 256
val tileHeight = 256
val srid = 4326

// Create an AffineTransform: scale 0.01, shear 0, 0, -0.01, minX -180, maxY 90
val g2m = new AffineTransform(0.01, 0.0, 0.0, -0.01, -180.0, 90.0)

// Construct the RasterMetadata
val meta = new RasterMetadata(x1, y1, x2, y2, tileWidth, tileHeight, srid, g2m)

// Verify the metadata properties match the input parameters
val isCorrect = (meta.x1 == x1) && 
                (meta.y1 == y1) && 
                (meta.x2 == x2) && 
                (meta.y2 == y2) && 
                (meta.tileWidth == tileWidth) && 
                (meta.tileHeight == tileHeight) && 
                (meta.srid == srid)

assert(isCorrect, "RasterMetadata properties do not match the provided constructor arguments")

// Witness: The total number of pixels in the defined extent
val totalPixels = (meta.x2 - meta.x1) * (meta.y2 - meta.y1)
println(s"__CHECK__ scala:edu.ucr.cs.bdlab.beast.geolite.RasterMetadata $totalPixels")

Exact standard output

__CHECK__ scala:edu.ucr.cs.bdlab.beast.geolite.RasterMetadata 462836
__DONE__ object=GeoJob

SedonaDB

Selected API: ST_Distance. Documentation condition: Generated README D1.

Actual input. Two planar points (0,0) and (3,4), followed by geographic points (0°,0°) and (0°,1°), created directly in the SQL expressions. No raster/vector file is consumed by these calls.

What the saved program checks. The generated program checks planar distance 5 within 1e−9 and geographic distance between 110,000 and 112,000.

Full prompt | LLM response | Complete executable program | Actual output | Errors/warnings

Execution command and time | Model time and tokens | Verdict and provenance

Fixture paths, hashes, and initialization

Exact returned fragment

table_geom = sd.sql("SELECT ST_Distance(ST_Point(0.0, 0.0), ST_Point(3.0, 4.0)) as dist").to_arrow_table()
dist_geom = table_geom.column("dist")[0].as_py()
assert abs(dist_geom - 5.0) < 1e-9
print(f"__CHECK__ sql:ST_Distance {dist_geom}")

table_geog = sd.sql("SELECT ST_Distance(ST_GeogPoint(0.0, 0.0), ST_GeogPoint(0.0, 1.0)) as dist").to_arrow_table()
dist_geog = table_geog.column("dist")[0].as_py()
# 1 degree of latitude is approximately 111,319 meters
assert dist_geog > 110000 and dist_geog < 112000
print(f"__CHECK__ sql:ST_Distance {dist_geog}")

Exact standard output

__CHECK__ sql:ST_Distance 5.0
__CHECK__ sql:ST_Distance 111195.10117748393
__DONE__

MDAnalysis

Selected API: calc_bonds. Documentation condition: Original documentation.

Actual input. The provided molecular Universe supplies its first five atoms and next five atoms as five coordinate pairs. The exact PSF/topology and DCD/trajectory paths and hashes appear in INPUTS.json.

What the saved program checks. The generated program checks an ndarray of shape (5,) and nonnegative distances. It does not check each distance against an independent numerical answer.

Full prompt | LLM response | Complete executable program | Actual output | Errors/warnings

Execution command and time | Model time and tokens | Verdict and provenance

Fixture paths, hashes, and initialization

Exact returned fragment

import MDAnalysis.lib.distances as dists
import numpy as np

# Use the preloaded universe and select two small groups of atoms
# to calculate bond lengths between corresponding pairs.
# We select the first 5 atoms and the next 5 atoms to form 5 pairs.
ag1 = universe.atoms[:5]
ag2 = universe.atoms[5:10]

# Calculate bond lengths
bond_lengths = dists.calc_bonds(ag1, ag2)

# Verify the result shape matches the number of pairs
assert bond_lengths.shape == (5,)
assert isinstance(bond_lengths, np.ndarray)

# Verify that the bond lengths are non-negative (physical constraint)
assert np.all(bond_lengths >= 0)

# Print witness: the number of bonds calculated and the mean bond length
print(f"__CHECK__ python:MDAnalysis.lib.distances.calc_bonds {bond_lengths.shape[0]} {np.mean(bond_lengths):.4f}")

Exact standard output

__CHECK__ python:MDAnalysis.lib.distances.calc_bonds 5 2.7657
__DONE__

tslearn

Selected API: dtw. Documentation condition: Generated README D1.

Actual input. The provided s1 and s2 series come from the pinned Trace NPZ fixture. The program also creates the tiny series [0,1] and [0,2].

What the saved program checks. It checks zero DTW distance for identical series, a nonnegative constrained distance, and distance 1 for the tiny explicit pair.

Full prompt | LLM response | Complete executable program | Actual output | Errors/warnings

Execution command and time | Model time and tokens | Verdict and provenance

Fixture paths, hashes, and initialization

Exact returned fragment

from tslearn.metrics import dtw
import numpy as np

# Use the provided 1D series s1 and s2
# DTW distance between identical series should be 0.0
dist_identical = dtw(s1, s1)
assert dist_identical == 0.0, f"Expected 0.0 for identical series, got {dist_identical}"

# Compute DTW with Sakoe-Chiba constraint
# Using a small radius on a subset of the provided data
s1_sub = s1[:10]
s2_sub = s2[:10]
dist_constrained = dtw(s1_sub, s2_sub, global_constraint="sakoe_chiba", sakoe_chiba_radius=2)

# Verify the result is a non-negative float
assert isinstance(dist_constrained, float), "Result must be a float"
assert dist_constrained >= 0.0, "Distance must be non-negative"

# Verify against a known simple case: distance between [0, 1] and [0, 2]
# Path: (0,0) -> (1,1) => sqrt((0-0)^2 + (1-2)^2) = 1.0
dist_simple = dtw([0, 1], [0, 2])
assert np.isclose(dist_simple, 1.0), f"Expected 1.0, got {dist_simple}"

print(f"__CHECK__ python:tslearn.metrics._dtw.dtw {dist_constrained:.4f}")

Exact standard output

__CHECK__ python:tslearn.metrics._dtw.dtw 0.1175
__DONE__

mir_eval

Selected API: intervals_to_durations. Documentation condition: Generated README D1.

Actual input. The preloaded ref_intervals is [[0,0.5],[0.5,1],[1,1.5],[1.5,2]]. No annotation file is needed for this particular example.

What the saved program checks. The generated program checks the output shape (four entries) and verifies the first duration equals its interval end minus start; it prints the duration sum.

Full prompt | LLM response | Complete executable program | Actual output | Errors/warnings

Execution command and time | Model time and tokens | Verdict and provenance

This example reuses the existing verified evidence files; no duplicate copy was made. The Original-condition comparison is alongside it in the existing intervals-to-durations/original folder.

Exact returned fragment

import numpy as np
import mir_eval.util

# Use the provided ref_intervals (shape (4, 2))
durations = mir_eval.util.intervals_to_durations(ref_intervals)

# Verify the shape matches the number of intervals
assert durations.shape == (4,)

# Verify the invariant: duration = end - start
# We check the first interval specifically
expected_first = ref_intervals[0, 1] - ref_intervals[0, 0]
assert np.isclose(durations[0], expected_first)

# Witness: print the sum of durations
total_duration = np.sum(durations)
print(f"__CHECK__ python:mir_eval.util.intervals_to_durations {total_duration}")

Exact standard output

__CHECK__ python:mir_eval.util.intervals_to_durations 2.0
__DONE__

Thumbnailator

Selected API: Thumbnails.Builder.asBufferedImage. Documentation condition: Generated README D1.

Actual input. The preamble creates a 32×24 RGB image with a blue background and a yellow rectangle. The generated program requests a 16×12 thumbnail.

What the saved program checks. It throws AssertionError unless the returned image is 16×12, then prints its center pixel. The pixel color is recorded, not asserted by this program.

Full prompt | LLM response | Complete executable program | Actual output | Errors/warnings

Execution command and time | Model time and tokens | Verdict and provenance

Fixture paths, hashes, and initialization

Exact returned fragment

java.awt.image.BufferedImage result = net.coobird.thumbnailator.Thumbnails.of(sourceImage)
    .size(16, 12)
    .asBufferedImage();

if (result.getWidth() != 16 || result.getHeight() != 12) {
    throw new AssertionError("Thumbnail dimensions mismatch: expected 16x12, got " + result.getWidth() + "x" + result.getHeight());
}

int pixel = result.getRGB(8, 6);
System.out.println("__CHECK__ java:net.coobird.thumbnailator.Thumbnails.Builder.asBufferedImage " + result.getWidth() + "x" + result.getHeight() + "@" + Integer.toHexString(pixel));

Exact standard output

__CHECK__ java:net.coobird.thumbnailator.Thumbnails.Builder.asBufferedImage 16x12@ffffff00
__DONE__

The CSV uses short attempt paths to avoid repeating long cluster paths thousands of times. To open a complete trace, take the corresponding original_run_directory or generated_run_directory from BANK_INDEX.json, append the CSV attempt path, then open request.json, response.txt, the named program, execution.json, stdout.txt, or stderr.txt. Blank attempt paths mean no recorded attempt was available in this snapshot. Pending MDAnalysis D1 rows are not failures.

For example, in a VS Code Remote SSH window connected to lab, open the exact attempt directory from the bank root and CSV. Its request.json contains the full request actually sent for that API. The six examples above are copied locally for immediate review; all other per-API prompts and outputs remain in their original cluster run directories. No large input datasets were copied.

What exists and what is still planned

Artifact Current state
Selected API inventory Complete local lists for all six libraries; RDPro has distinct Scala and Java banks.
Actual R0 model prompts, snippets, console outputs, and native verdicts Recorded per attempted target in the linked run directories; six representative traces are available locally above.
Fixed independently authored microbenchmark tasks with expected outputs Not supplied by this current native API-evaluation cohort. The model chooses a small check from the target API, supplied documentation, and available inputs.
Current puzzle bank Not executed in this cohort. The six current configurations refer to integration_tasks.yaml paths that were absent when this index was captured; exact paths and existence checks are in BANK_INDEX.json. This R0 path does not read those files.
R1/R2 program-repair traces Not present for these R0-only runs.
Generated README revision D2 and remaining ablations Separate planned or later stages; no measurements are invented here.

Historical puzzle banks elsewhere in the project may remain useful, but they must be reviewed, versioned, and bound to a new study before being presented as the puzzle bank for this protocol. A manifest of API names is not a substitute for fixed task inputs and independently checked expected answers.

Storage and verification

Only small trace files and API indexes were copied. INPUTS.json links the original fixtures and records their hashes; the complete wrapped program preserves any synthetic inputs. Full prompts are copied exactly, rather than reconstructed from a newer template. The main examples reuse the existing mir_eval evidence and leave earlier Java evidence untouched.

import java.awt.geom.AffineTransform

// Define parameters for a 737x628 raster (matching the provided rasterRDD dimensions)
val x1 = 0
val y1 = 0
val x2 = 737
val y2 = 628
val tileWidth = 256
val tileHeight = 256
val srid = 4326

// Create an AffineTransform: scale 0.01, shear 0, 0, -0.01, minX -180, maxY 90
val g2m = new AffineTransform(0.01, 0.0, 0.0, -0.01, -180.0, 90.0)

// Construct the RasterMetadata
val meta = new RasterMetadata(x1, y1, x2, y2, tileWidth, tileHeight, srid, g2m)

// Verify the metadata properties match the input parameters
val isCorrect = (meta.x1 == x1) && 
                (meta.y1 == y1) && 
                (meta.x2 == x2) && 
                (meta.y2 == y2) && 
                (meta.tileWidth == tileWidth) && 
                (meta.tileHeight == tileHeight) && 
                (meta.srid == srid)

assert(isCorrect, "RasterMetadata properties do not match the provided constructor arguments")

// Witness: The total number of pixels in the defined extent

println(s"__CHECK__ scala:edu.ucr.cs.bdlab.beast.geolite.RasterMetadata $totalPixels")

// Compiled form (scalac + spark-submit --class GeoJobMain). This is the proven
// path: an implicit class like RaptorMixin's sc.geoTiff resolves when compiled
// inside an object, but NOT in the spark-shell -i REPL.
object GeoJob {
  def run(sc: SparkContext): Unit = {
    // Alias: LLM-authored snippets frequently reach for `sparkContext` (the
    // SparkSession accessor name) rather than the harness binding `sc`. Expose
    // both so a correct call doesn't fail on the binding name alone.
    val sparkContext = sc
    val spark = SparkSession.builder().getOrCreate()
    // Typed sample inputs (from comprehension.execute.sample_data). Use the
    // one(s) whose type matches the API's parameters.
    val vector_shapefile = "file:///home/zshan011/apps/aideal/studies/2026-11-17-full-rdpro/inputs/fixtures/Boston_Neighborhood_Boundaries_sample_grail.shp"
    val raster_tif = "file:///home/zshan011/apps/aideal/studies/2026-11-17-full-rdpro/inputs/fixtures/nldas_boston_30m.tif"
    val hdf_file = "file:///home/zshan011/apps/aideal/studies/2026-11-17-full-rdpro/inputs/fixtures/sample.hdf"
    val output_dir = "file:///home/zshan011/apps/aideal/studies/2026-11-17-latest-stable-canonical/evaluator-correction-2026-11-18/explicit-inputs-r0/continuation/rdpro_scala/generated_d1/execution/run_scala_edu.ucr.cs.bdlab.beast.geolite.RasterMetadata--6d17178d7882e606f753b657dc9d2cdfffaaa36ede596f6dc6e640b654eee892/attempt_0001/output"

    // pre-loaded typed inputs (comprehension.execute.preamble)
    // Fixed input contract shared by all conditions: the supplied GeoTIFF has11Float32bands.
    val rasterRDD: RasterRDD[Array[Float]] = sc.geoTiff[Array[Float]](raster_tif)
    val featuresRDD: RDD[IFeature] = sc.shapefile(vector_shapefile)

    // TODO API_TEST_START
    import edu.ucr.cs.bdlab.beast.geolite.RasterMetadata
    import java.awt.geom.AffineTransform

    // Define parameters for a 737x628 raster (matching the provided rasterRDD dimensions)
    val x1 = 0
    val y1 = 0
    val x2 = 737
    val y2 = 628
    val tileWidth = 256
    val tileHeight = 256
    val srid = 4326

    // Create an AffineTransform: scale 0.01, shear 0, 0, -0.01, minX -180, maxY 90
    val g2m = new AffineTransform(0.01, 0.0, 0.0, -0.01, -180.0, 90.0)

    // Construct the RasterMetadata
    val meta = new RasterMetadata(x1, y1, x2, y2, tileWidth, tileHeight, srid, g2m)

    // Verify the metadata properties match the input parameters
    val isCorrect = (meta.x1 == x1) && 
                    (meta.y1 == y1) && 
                    (meta.x2 == x2) && 
                    (meta.y2 == y2) && 
                    (meta.tileWidth == tileWidth) && 
                    (meta.tileHeight == tileHeight) && 
                    (meta.srid == srid)

    assert(isCorrect, "RasterMetadata properties do not match the provided constructor arguments")

    // Witness: The total number of pixels in the defined extent
    val totalPixels = (meta.x2 - meta.x1) * (meta.y2 - meta.y1)
    println(s"__CHECK__ scala:edu.ucr.cs.bdlab.beast.geolite.RasterMetadata $totalPixels")
    // TODO API_TEST_END
  }
}

object GeoJobMain {
  def main(args: Array[String]): Unit = {
    val spark = SparkSession.builder().appName("ApiTest").master("local[2]").getOrCreate()
    try {
      GeoJob.run(spark.sparkContext)
      println("__DONE__ object=GeoJob")
    } catch {
      case t: Throwable =>
        Console.err.println("__RUN_ERR__ " + t.getClass.getName + ": " + t.getMessage)
        t.printStackTrace()
        throw t
    } finally {
      spark.stop()
    }
  }
}

table_geom = sd.sql("SELECT ST_Distance(ST_Point(0.0, 0.0), ST_Point(3.0, 4.0)) as dist").to_arrow_table()
dist_geom = table_geom.column("dist")[0].as_py()
assert abs(dist_geom - 5.0) < 1e-9
print(f"__CHECK__ sql:ST_Distance {dist_geom}")

table_geog = sd.sql("SELECT ST_Distance(ST_GeogPoint(0.0, 0.0), ST_GeogPoint(0.0, 1.0)) as dist").to_arrow_table()
dist_geog = table_geog.column("dist")[0].as_py()
# 1 degree of latitude is approximately 111,319 meters
assert dist_geog > 110000 and dist_geog < 112000
print(f"__CHECK__ sql:ST_Distance {dist_geog}")
ApiTest.py
from pathlib import Path
import math
import json
from sedona_runtime import TrackedContext, requested_sql_target


def main(*, Path=Path, TrackedContext=TrackedContext, json=json, math=math, requested_sql_target=requested_sql_target):
    _, target_api = requested_sql_target(__file__)
    sd = TrackedContext(target_api)
    _tracked_connection = sd
    raster_cea_tif = r"/home/zshan011/apps/aideal/studies/2026-11-16-six-library-gemini-flash-lite/libraries/sedonadb/fixtures/cea.tif"
    raster_test4_tif = r"/home/zshan011/apps/aideal/studies/2026-11-16-six-library-gemini-flash-lite/libraries/sedonadb/fixtures/test4.tiff"
    output_dir = r"/home/zshan011/apps/aideal/studies/2026-11-17-latest-stable-canonical/evaluator-correction-2026-11-18/explicit-inputs-r0/continuation/sedonadb/generated_d1/execution/run_sql_ST_Distance--8bed70b7baaedb77e88e151d5fb938563729570191d96e27fb565ca1ab1eebd5/attempt_0001/output"
    # TODO API_TEST_START
    table_geom = sd.sql("SELECT ST_Distance(ST_Point(0.0, 0.0), ST_Point(3.0, 4.0)) as dist").to_arrow_table()
    dist_geom = table_geom.column("dist")[0].as_py()
    assert abs(dist_geom - 5.0) < 1e-9
    print(f"__CHECK__ sql:ST_Distance {dist_geom}")

    table_geog = sd.sql("SELECT ST_Distance(ST_GeogPoint(0.0, 0.0), ST_GeogPoint(0.0, 1.0)) as dist").to_arrow_table()
    dist_geog = table_geog.column("dist")[0].as_py()
    # 1 degree of latitude is approximately 111,319 meters
    assert dist_geog > 110000 and dist_geog < 112000
    print(f"__CHECK__ sql:ST_Distance {dist_geog}")
    # TODO API_TEST_END
    _tracked_connection.verify()
    print("__DONE__")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

:target: https://github.com/MDAnalysis/mdanalysis/discussions\n\n\n===== https://docs.mdanalysis.org/2.11.0/documentation_pages/lib/distances.html#MDAnalysis.lib.distances.calc_bonds =====\nMDAnalysis.lib.distances.calc_bonds(coords1: ndarray[tuple[Any, ...], dtype[_ScalarT]] | AtomGroup, coords2: ndarray[tuple[Any, ...], dtype[_ScalarT]] | AtomGroup, box: ndarray[tuple[Any, ...], dtype[_ScalarT]] | None = None, result: ndarray[tuple[Any, ...], dtype[_ScalarT]] | None = None, backend: str = 'serial') → ndarray[tuple[Any, ...], dtype[_ScalarT]][source]\n\nCalculates the bond lengths between pairs of atom positions from the two coordinate arrays coords1 and coords2, which must contain the same number of coordinates. coords1[i] and coords2[i] represent the positions of atoms connected by the i-th bond. If single coordinates are supplied, a single distance will be returned.\n\nIn comparison to distance_array() and self_distance_array(), which calculate distances between all possible combinations of coordinates, calc_bonds() only calculates distances between pairs of coordinates, similar to:\n\nnumpy.linalg.norm(a - b) for a, b in zip(coords1, coords2)\n\nIf the optional argument box is supplied, the minimum image convention is applied when calculating distances. Either orthogonal or triclinic boxes are supported.\n\nIf a numpy array of dtype numpy.float64 with shape (n,) (for n coordinate pairs) is provided in result, then this preallocated array is filled. This can speed up calculations.\n\nParameters:\n\ncoords1 (numpy.ndarray or AtomGroup) – Coordinate array of shape (3,) or (n, 3) for one half of a single or n bonds, respectively (dtype is arbitrary, will be converted to numpy.float32 internally). Also accepts an AtomGroup.\n\ncoords2 (numpy.ndarray or AtomGroup) – Coordinate array of shape (3,) or (n, 3) for the other half of a single or n bonds, respectively (dtype is arbitrary, will be converted to numpy.float32 internally). Also accepts an AtomGroup.\n\nbox (numpy.ndarray, optional) – The unitcell dimensions of the system, which can be orthogonal or triclinic and must be provided in the same format as returned by MDAnalysis.coordinates.timestep.Timestep.dimensions: [lx, ly, lz, alpha, beta, gamma].\n\nresult (numpy.ndarray, optional) – Preallocated result array of dtype numpy.float64 and shape (n,) (for n coordinate pairs). Avoids recreating the array in repeated function calls.\n\nbackend ({'serial', 'OpenMP', 'distopia'}, optional) – Keyword selecting the type of acceleration. Defaults to ‘serial’.\n\nReturns:\n\nbondlengths – numpy.float64 Array containing the bond lengths between each pair of coordinates. If two single coordinates were supplied, their distance is returned as a single number instead of an array.\n\nReturn type:\n\nnumpy.ndarray (dtype=numpy.float64, shape=(n,)) or\n\nAdded in version 0.8.\n\nChanged in version 0.13.0: Added backend keyword.\n\nChanged in version 0.19.0: Internal dtype conversion of input coordinates to numpy.float32. Now also accepts single coordinates as input.\n\nChanged in version 2.3.0: Can now accept an AtomGroup as an argument in any position and checks inputs using type hinting.\n\nChanged in version 2.5.0: Can now optionally use the fast distance functions from distopia\n\n\nAvailable input path variables already in scope (choose the matching one(s) by type):\nPre-loaded and already in scope — use these directly, do NOT re-load:\n- MDAnalysis: module\n- numpy: module\n- universe: MDAnalysis.Universe\n- atoms: MDAnalysis.core.groups.AtomGroup\n- protein: MDAnalysis.core.groups.AtomGroup\n- backbone: MDAnalysis.core.groups.AtomGroup\n- trajectory: MDAnalysis.coordinates.base.ReaderBase\n- timestep: MDAnalysis.coordinates.base.Timestep\n(raw input path strings also in scope if needed: psf_path, dcd_path, pdb_path, gro_path, xtc_path, mdanalysis_fixture_dir, tng_path, h5md_path, mmcif_path, gsd_path, tpr_path, edr_path, xvg_path, fasta_path, imd_trr_path, small_pdb_path, dna_pdb_path, fixture_catalog_path, chemfiles_trajectory_path, output_dir)\nWritable output_dir is a directory unique to this attempt; keep generated files beneath it.\n\nRECEIVER — call `python:MDAnalysis.lib.distances.calc_bonds` on the RIGHT type (calling it on the wrong receiver is\nthe #1 failure; if it is an instance method, first obtain a value of the owner type,\ndo NOT call it on an unrelated preloaded input):\n(receiver type not resolved)\n\n(none yet)\n\nWrite minimal body statements that use `python:MDAnalysis.lib.distances.calc_bonds` correctly per the docs,\nreading from the appropriate input variable(s) above.\n\nThe snippet MUST EXECUTE the operation AND VERIFY the result is correct — not just\nthat it ran (some frameworks defer/lazily evaluate, and incorrectly interpreted\ndata can produce plausible output without throwing). import MDAnalysis.lib.distances as dists
import numpy as np

# Use the preloaded universe and select two small groups of atoms
# to calculate bond lengths between corresponding pairs.
# We select the first 5 atoms and the next 5 atoms to form 5 pairs.
ag1 = universe.atoms[:5]
ag2 = universe.atoms[5:10]

# Calculate bond lengths
bond_lengths = dists.calc_bonds(ag1, ag2)

# Verify the result shape matches the number of pairs
assert bond_lengths.shape == (5,)
assert isinstance(bond_lengths, np.ndarray)

# Verify that the bond lengths are non-negative (physical constraint)
assert np.all(bond_lengths >= 0)

# Print witness: the number of bonds calculated and the mean bond length
print(f"__CHECK__ python:MDAnalysis.lib.distances.calc_bonds {bond_lengths.shape[0]} {np.mean(bond_lengths):.4f}")

response.txt
from tslearn.metrics import dtw
import numpy as np

# Use the provided 1D series s1 and s2
# DTW distance between identical series should be 0.0
dist_identical = dtw(s1, s1)
assert dist_identical == 0.0, f"Expected 0.0 for identical series, got {dist_identical}"

# Compute DTW with Sakoe-Chiba constraint
# Using a small radius on a subset of the provided data
s1_sub = s1[:10]
s2_sub = s2[:10]
dist_constrained = dtw(s1_sub, s2_sub, global_constraint="sakoe_chiba", sakoe_chiba_radius=2)

# Verify the result is a non-negative float
assert isinstance(dist_constrained, float), "Result must be a float"
assert dist_constrained >= 0.0, "Distance must be non-negative"

# Verify against a known simple case: distance between [0, 1] and [0, 2]
# Path: (0,0) -> (1,1) => sqrt((0-0)^2 + (1-2)^2) = 1.0
dist_simple = dtw([0, 1], [0, 2])
assert np.isclose(dist_simple, 1.0), f"Expected 1.0, got {dist_simple}"

print(f"__CHECK__ python:tslearn.metrics._dtw.dtw {dist_constrained:.4f}")

from pathlib import Path

import traceback

import numpy as np


def run():
    # Typed repository fixture path and condition-specific output directory:
    trace_npz = r"/home/zshan011/apps/aideal/studies/2026-11-17-latest-stable-canonical/sources/tslearn/tslearn/.cached_datasets/Trace.npz"
    output_dir = r"/home/zshan011/apps/aideal/studies/2026-11-17-latest-stable-canonical/evaluator-correction-2026-11-18/explicit-inputs-r0/continuation/tslearn/generated_d1/execution/run_python_tslearn.metrics._dtw.dtw--1030f6652ac41edcbb4e216b7eeb1c69a54f12c8b6a7ac83287e9655cc75932b/attempt_0001/output"

    _trace = np.load(trace_npz)
    _balanced = np.array([0, 4, 1, 17, 3, 13, 2, 5], dtype=int)
    X = _trace["X_train"][_balanced, :40, :]  # type: balanced time-series dataset ndarray (8, 40, 1)
    X2 = _trace["X_test"][:4, :32, :]  # type: second dataset ndarray (4, 32, 1)
    s1 = X[0].ravel()  # type: 1-D series ndarray (40,)
    s2 = X[1].ravel()  # type: 1-D series ndarray (40,)
    y = _trace["y_train"][_balanced]  # type: four balanced labels, two examples per class
    tiny_matrix = np.arange(12.0).reshape(3, 4)  # type: deterministic numeric matrix

    # Keep audience imports local so they cannot shadow fixture construction.
    def api_test(*, Path=Path, X=X, X2=X2, _balanced=_balanced, _trace=_trace, np=np, output_dir=output_dir, s1=s1, s2=s2, sys=sys, tiny_matrix=tiny_matrix, trace_npz=trace_npz, traceback=traceback, y=y):
        # TODO API_TEST_START
        from tslearn.metrics import dtw
        import numpy as np

        # Use the provided 1D series s1 and s2
        # DTW distance between identical series should be 0.0
        dist_identical = dtw(s1, s1)
        assert dist_identical == 0.0, f"Expected 0.0 for identical series, got {dist_identical}"

        # Compute DTW with Sakoe-Chiba constraint
        # Using a small radius on a subset of the provided data
        s1_sub = s1[:10]
        s2_sub = s2[:10]
        dist_constrained = dtw(s1_sub, s2_sub, global_constraint="sakoe_chiba", sakoe_chiba_radius=2)

        # Verify the result is a non-negative float
        assert isinstance(dist_constrained, float), "Result must be a float"
        assert dist_constrained >= 0.0, "Distance must be non-negative"

        # Verify against a known simple case: distance between [0, 1] and [0, 2]
        # Path: (0,0) -> (1,1) => sqrt((0-0)^2 + (1-2)^2) = 1.0
        dist_simple = dtw([0, 1], [0, 2])
        assert np.isclose(dist_simple, 1.0), f"Expected 1.0, got {dist_simple}"

        print(f"__CHECK__ python:tslearn.metrics._dtw.dtw {dist_constrained:.4f}")
        # TODO API_TEST_END
        pass

    api_test()

def run():
    beat_reference_file = r"/home/zshan011/apps/aideal/studies/2026-11-17-latest-stable-canonical/sources/mir_eval/tests/data/beat/ref00.txt"
    beat_estimate_file = r"/home/zshan011/apps/aideal/studies/2026-11-17-latest-stable-canonical/sources/mir_eval/tests/data/beat/est00.txt"

    # pre-loaded typed inputs (comprehension.execute.preamble)
    times = np.array([0.0, 0.5, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0])  # type: numpy.ndarray event/frame times
    ref_events = np.array([0.0, 0.5, 1.0, 1.5])  # type: numpy.ndarray reference events
    est_events = np.array([0.02, 0.48, 1.03, 1.52])  # type: numpy.ndarray estimated events

    est_intervals = np.array([[0.0, 0.48], [0.48, 1.02], [1.02, 1.48], [1.48, 2.0]])  # type: numpy.ndarray estimated intervals
    ref_labels = ["C:maj", "G:maj", "A:min", "F:maj"]  # type: list[str] reference labels
    est_labels = ["C:maj", "G:maj", "A:min", "F:maj"]  # type: list[str] estimated labels

    est_freqs = np.array([442.0, 438.0, 0.0, 525.0, 520.0])  # type: numpy.ndarray estimated frequencies
    ref_sources = np.vstack([np.sin(2*np.pi*3*times), np.cos(2*np.pi*2*times)])  # type: numpy.ndarray reference sources
    est_sources = ref_sources + 0.001  # type: numpy.ndarray estimated sources

    def api_test(*, alignment=alignment, beat=beat, beat_estimate_file=beat_estimate_file, beat_reference_file=beat_reference_file, chord=chord, chord_reference_file=chord_reference_file, display=display, est_events=est_events, est_freqs=est_freqs, est_intervals=est_intervals, est_labels=est_labels, est_sources=est_sources, hierarchy=hierarchy, io=io, key=key, melody=melody, melody_reference_file=melody_reference_file, mir_eval=mir_eval, multipitch=multipitch, np=np, onset=onset, output_dir=output_dir, pattern=pattern, ref_events=ref_events, ref_freqs=ref_freqs, ref_intervals=ref_intervals, ref_labels=ref_labels, ref_sources=ref_sources, segment=segment, separation=separation, sonify=sonify, sys=sys, tempo=tempo, times=times, traceback=traceback, transcription=transcription, transcription_velocity=transcription_velocity, util=util):
        # TODO API_TEST_START
        import numpy as np
        import mir_eval.util

        # Use the provided ref_intervals (shape (4, 2))
        durations = mir_eval.util.intervals_to_durations(ref_intervals)

        # Verify the shape matches the number of intervals
        assert durations.shape == (4,)

        # Verify the invariant: duration = end - start
        # We check the first interval specifically

        assert np.isclose(durations[0], expected_first)
